I read the whole order; the representation is the machine-extracted text layer of the official PDF (OCR for this period), and I did not inspect the page images. End: express revocation by Executive Order 12553 on 1986-02-25, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines (option C): a report deadline and a spent standstill are not ends. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the dossier; the U.S. Code and eCFR searches found no hit naming this order. The held-text number search for this order found no held later order that cites it other than Executive Order 12553.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its commands are a bar on interested board members, a requirement that the board report within thirty days from the date of the order (due by February 12, 1969), and a standstill running from the date of the order until thirty days after the report. The report deadline and the standstill are spent facts, not ends, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and neither is claimed as the end. No report of the board created by this order is held among the sources cited by the dossier, so no report date and no end date of the standstill is recorded; the clause is described only as functionally spent because its report deadline (due by February 12, 1969) and statutory standstill are completed-in-time commands, with no held source fixing a date. My reading of the held texts of Executive Order 12553 and the number search of the held texts show no earlier revoking, superseding or terminating instrument for this order.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on this order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence applies section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); the statute is a separate source of law that an Executive order does not revoke, but the periods measured here were set by this order's creation of the board, and no held claim-grade source shows them still running when the order was revoked.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full: it consists of one revoking sentence, a table of the revoked orders by number and subject, an editorial note and the signature block, and it contains no saving or transition provision. No residual effect of this order is shown to depend on its continued force. Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (the recitals, the creation of the board of three members, the bar on interested members, the report requirement and the standstill). The signature, the date line and any attached lists follow it; the attached lists, where present, only identify carriers and labor organizations and contain no command. Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number as a whole, not in part, so every command in the clause ends on the same date. I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11444.txt, 34 FR 543, PDF pages 7-8) and confirmed that its printed number, title and signature date match the census register row. The order has one operative paragraph with no numbered sections and no stated end date, so the dossier's single whole-order clause is the complete inventory (any carrier list the order attaches is an incorporated list, not a separate command). EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25; held text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt) revokes the order by number in its table. The first end is that express revocation. The report deadline, the report itself and the end of the standstill are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and no held reviewed source establishes the dates on which the board reported or the standstill ran out. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order states a report deadline (\\\"within thirty days from the date of this order\\\") and a standstill running (\\\"for thirty days after the board has made its report\\\"). A report deadline is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill is a spent fact whose date no held source fixes; neither is recorded as an end. The order states no date on which it ends. Held later instruments: the by-number scan lists no held later order that cites this order other than EO 12553, which names it in its revocation table; the Federal Register catalog note records the EO 12553 revocation and the U.S. Code and eCFR searches return no hit. No unnamed statute or order was found that would be an earlier candidate end beyond section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, the statute the order itself invokes.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order (1) creates the board and bars members with a financial or organizational interest, (2) directs a report within thirty days and (3) recites the section 10 standstill. Command (3) rests on the statute, which binds the parties for its own thirty-day period regardless of the order, so it is not an independent surviving command of the order. The order makes no appointment by name, creates no fund or property right and delegates no continuing authority, so nothing in it outlives its revocation.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: no savings language in EO 12553 or in this order; the completed facts are spent and nothing continues independently.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole-order scope: EO 12553 revokes the order in full. Its table lists the order by number with no 'in part' or section limitation, so the single clause ends on the revocation date, and the held sources record no partial amendment of this order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11444#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11444#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"eb3cc827ec6555074cbb64300ab64405afdc15d9909b7a997c27c8ea3509a05b","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11444.txt","sha256":"eb3cc827ec6555074cbb64300ab64405afdc15d9909b7a997c27c8ea3509a05b","state":"match"},"sha256":"b59fa935f2126e0289c24caec5cf18378a0292089f264caf273c5650c44794b6","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11444.txt","text_sha256":"eb3cc827ec6555074cbb64300ab64405afdc15d9909b7a997c27c8ea3509a05b"}]}]}

Establish occurrence from evidence and explain its effect on this clause."],"scope_id":"EO:11445#whole","state":"requires_clause_specific_research"}],"existing_lifecycle_questions_notice":"Current dossier research prompts, not new gap findings or overrides of prior claim/review conclusions.","new_legal_gaps_invented":[],"prior_differences_require_scope_source_proposition_review":true},"selection_and_checkpoint_records":[],"state":"prior_work_located","status_holds":[],"status_research":[],"unattached_status_reviews":[],"unfinished_work":[],"versions":[{"claim_sha256":"81cc693973cb8ae682684a702a45ebc92c427bb3149f7cf90147b4a15fdb2024","current_canonical":true,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"4093fecd9f9e1de3d87de70a639d4deacdc562bacdfdd386c65beb54d2d43b73","path":"research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-10-31-apollo-g08.jsonl","pointer":"line:14"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 11445 (1969-01-13, 34 FR 545) creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate disputes between the carriers represented by the National Railway Labor Conference and certain of their employees represented by the Brotherhood of Railroad Signalmen, directs the board to report within thirty days, and recites the statutory standstill that section 10 imposes on the parties from the order's date until thirty days after the report. I read the full held Federal Register text (jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11445.txt, 34 FR 545, PDF pages 9-10) and confirmed that its printed number, title and signature date match the census register row. The order has one operative paragraph with no numbered sections and no stated end date, so the dossier's single whole-order clause is the complete inventory (any carrier list the order attaches is an incorporated list, not a separate command). EO 12553 (signed 1986-02-25; held text jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt) revokes the order by number in its table. The first end is that express revocation. The report deadline, the report itself and the end of the standstill are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and no held reviewed source establishes the dates on which the board reported or the standstill ran out. Bounded search read for this order: the dossier's three machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121 through 2026-10-09; eCFR and GovInfo CFR bulk through 2026-10-14; Federal Register catalog disposition notes and operative sentences of 17,521 saved order texts), after reading their coverage notes, which carry no coverage warning; the Cruden by-number scan of held order texts in the packet's LOOKUPS.md; and a full read of EO 12553 (held FR text, 33,227 characters).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own terms: the order states a report deadline (\\\"within thirty days from the date of this order\\\") and a standstill running (\\\"for thirty days after the board has made its report\\\"). A report deadline is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill is a spent fact whose date no held source fixes; neither is recorded as an end. The order states no date on which it ends. Held later instruments: the by-number scan lists no held later order that cites this order other than EO 12553, which names it in its revocation table; the Federal Register catalog note records the EO 12553 revocation and the U.S. Code and eCFR searches return no hit. No unnamed statute or order was found that would be an earlier candidate end beyond section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, the statute the order itself invokes.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order (1) creates the board and bars members with a financial or organizational interest, (2) directs a report within thirty days and (3) recites the section 10 standstill. Command (3) rests on the statute, which binds the parties for its own thirty-day period regardless of the order, so it is not an independent surviving command of the order.
